### ` Non Awarded Vendor Request Form 7.2025.pdf`

- **tags:** `non-awarded-vendor-request-form`, `procurement-justification`, `vendor-compliance`, `purchasing-guidelines`, `district-contract-requirements`, `goods-and-services-request`, `endorse-form-7.2025`, `procurement-services-submission`
- **entities:** `agreements@dallasisd.org`, `Non-Awarded Vendor Request Form`
- **artifacts:** `form-template`, `instructional-document`, `procurement-form`, `checklist`, `submission-guidelines`
- **summary:** This document is the official Non-Awarded Vendor Request Form (version 7.2025) used by Dallas ISD end users to request approval for purchasing goods or services from a vendor without an existing district contract. It provides detailed instructions for completing the form, including required requestor and vendor information, specifications for the goods or services, and a critical justification section explaining why an awarded vendor cannot meet the need. The form must be submitted with supporting documentation to agreements@dallasisd.org and is subject to review by Procurement Services before any purchase order can be issued.

- **summary:** This document is the 2026 Summer Professional Development schedule for Dallas ISD Career and Technical Education staff, outlining a multi-day training event held June 11, 2026. It organizes sessions by time and room, covering topics such as AI tools (Gemini/NotebookLM), industry certification prep (Certiport, iCEV), lab safety, CTSO management, Google Workspace integration, and procurement procedures. The schedule targets teachers, coordinators, and campus admins, blending instructional technology, operational compliance, and student-centered programming like school store management and CTSO fundraising. It serves as both a day-of agenda and a reference for required trainings across CTE program families.

- **summary:** This guidebook provides the structured framework and step-by-step process for Texas local education agencies to complete the Comprehensive Local Needs Assessment (CLNA) required under Perkins V during the 2024-2025 school year. It outlines the seven assessment sections—from student performance and labor market alignment to equity and educator recruitment—while detailing stakeholder engagement requirements, data sources available through the TEAL system, and how CLNA findings must drive local application development and Perkins spending decisions.

### `26-27 CLNA.pdf`

- **tags:** `clna-2026-27`, `perkins-v`, `comprehensive-local-needs-assessment`, `dallas-isd-cte`, `workforce-development-area-6`, `llp-local-levels-of-performance`, `career-institutes`, `cte-program-evaluation`, `labor-market-alignment`, `student-performance-gaps`, `special-populations-analysis`, `gender-race-disaggregation`, `programs-of-study-tracker`, `budget-69-3m-cte`
- **entities:** `Dallas Independent School District`, `Greater Dallas Workforce Development Area 6`, `TEAL`, `CTER Perkins Summary`, `CTER Supplemental Report`, `Class of 2025`, `Class of 2026`, `Career Institute North`, `Career Institute South`, `Career Institute East`, `Townview Education Center`, `Carter High School`
- **artifacts:** `clna-draft`, `needs-assessment-document`, `performance-tracker`, `labor-market-analysis`, `program-evaluation`, `data-submission-template`, `budget-overview`
- **summary:** This is the Dallas ISD draft Comprehensive Local Needs Assessment (CLNA) for Perkins V, prepared May 2026 and pending TEAL/CTER data insertion and coordinator review. It structures the district's needs across five parts—student performance, labor market alignment, programs of study, special populations, and gender/race equity—using a dual-section format of submission text (for the Perkins application) and supporting analysis (for coordinator review). The document identifies specific performance gaps (notably Reading/ELA, postsecondary credential attainment, and special population underperformance), labor market misalignments (Health Science and Architecture/Construction underenrolled relative to demand), and strategic responses including Career Institute expansion, LLPs for local growth targets, and targeted recruitment for underrepresented groups. It serves as the central needs-analysis artifact guiding the district's CTE allocation of $69.3M and program design for the 2026-27 school year.

- **artifacts:** `federal-register-notice`, `policy-guidance`, `data-collection-specification`, `performance-reporting-framework`, `comment-request-document`
- **summary:** This September 11, 2024 Federal Register notice from the U.S. Department of Education’s Office of Career, Technical, and Adult Education proposes revisions to two principal Perkins V information collections—the State Plan Guide and the Consolidated Annual Report—to improve alignment of performance measurement with statutory requirements. The proposal addresses inconsistencies in how states identify CTE concentrators and report academic proficiency, suggesting a shift from active cohorts to exit-cohort reporting to reduce duplicative reporting and better align with Title I assessment practices. The document seeks public comment on changes intended to promote greater data consistency across states, fill information gaps in Perkins V administration, and inform future rulemaking.

### `CTE-Standard Operating Procedures/2026-27 Dallas ISD CTE CTSO Handbook.docx`

- **tags:** `ctso-handbook-2026-27`, `curriculum_admin`, `ctso-overview`, `chapter-establishment`, `advisor-responsibilities`, `fundraising-guidelines`, `competition-eligibility`, `field-trip-procedures`, `travel-guidelines`, `student-behavior-policies`, `stipend-requirements`, `ctso-2026-27`, `district-handbook`, `career-and-technical-student-organizations`
- **entities:** `Dallas ISD`, `Business Professionals of America (BPA)`, `DECA`, `Future Business Leaders of America (FBLA)`, `Family`, `Career and Community Leaders of America (FCCLA)`, `Health Occupations Students of America (HOSA)`, `SkillsUSA Texas`, `Texas Association of Future Educators (TAFE)`, `Texas FFA Association`, `Texas Technology Students Association (TSA)`, `Future Coaches of Texas Association`
- **artifacts:** `handbook`, `policy-document`, `procedures-manual`, `guide`, `reference-material`
- **summary:** This handbook serves as the official district-wide guide for Career and Technical Student Organizations (CTSOs) in Dallas ISD for the 2026-27 school year. It outlines the structure, goals, and operational procedures for establishing and maintaining CTSO chapters, covering advisor responsibilities, chapter requirements, membership dues, fundraising, competition eligibility, field trip protocols, travel guidelines, student behavior expectations, and advisor stipend requirements. The document lists all recognized CTSOs (including DECA, HOSA, SkillsUSA, FFA, FBLA, FCCLA, and others) and provides benchmarks, point structures, and safety guidelines to ensure consistent implementation across the district. It is intended for CTE teachers, campus administrators, and CTE coordinators to align CTSO activities with curriculum and district standards.

### `CTE-Standard Operating Procedures/Campus Lead Teacher Program.pdf`

- **tags:** `campus-lead-teacher`, `cte-lead-teacher-program`, `instructional-leadership`, `district-plc-facilitation`, `internship-coordination`, `work-based-learning`, `program-accountability`, `stipend-eligibility`, `compliance-deliverables`, `annual-evaluation`, `cte-initiative-implementation`
- **entities:** `Dallas ISD Career and Technical Education`, `District CTE Department Coordinator`, `Internship Expo`, `Eduthings`
- **artifacts:** `standard operating procedures`, `program manual`, `stipend tracker`, `evaluation rubric`, `policy document`
- **summary:** This document defines the Campus Lead Teacher role within Dallas ISD Career and Technical Education, outlining instructional, operational, and accountability responsibilities tied to a $2,500 annual stipend. It establishes one Lead Teacher per comprehensive high school (with exceptions for campuses with five or more Programs of Study) and ties stipend eligibility to measurable benchmarks in PLC facilitation, internship participation, industry-based certification reporting, and compliance with district deliverables. The role functions as a campus coordinator for work-based learning across all CTE programs while serving as a liaison between campus administration and the district CTE office.

It introduces and models Kagan cooperative learning structures—such as Stand Up-Hand Up-Pair Up, Numbered Heads Together, Timed Pair Share, Inside-Outside Circle, and Quiz-Quiz-Trade—with the goal of helping CTE instructors increase student engagement, accountability, and employability skills. The session connects cooperative learning strategies to CCMR goals, classroom management, and the "Skills for Success, Careers for Life!" framework, providing planning time, tips, and reflection prompts for teachers to implement the structures in their classrooms.

- **entities:** `Dallas ISD EMS Program`, `Career Institutes North`, `Career Institutes South`, `Baylor Scott & White`, `UT Southwestern`, `Parkland`, `Children's`, `Dallas Fire Department`, `Acadian Ambulance`, `CareFlite`, `NREMT`, `TxDSHS`
- **artifacts:** `counselor-reference-document`, `eligibility-profile`, `quick-reference-checklist`, `program-admissions-guide`, `policy-documentation`
- **summary:** This document is the official Dallas ISD EMS Program Student Eligibility Profile and Counselor Reference Guide, designed to help high school counselors determine student readiness for the two-year Emergency Medical Responder (EMR) to Emergency Medical Technician (EMT) pathway. It details academic prerequisites, age and licensure requirements, immunization and physical health standards, drug testing, background checks, clinical rotation commitments (including hospital and ambulance placements), and conduct policies such as HIPAA compliance. The document serves as both an advising tool for counselors and a pre-enrollment checklist for students and families, connecting the program's academic sequence with state certification logistics and clinical partner coordination.

- **summary:** This is a Vehicle Intake & Liability Waiver Form used by the Dallas ISD CTE Automotive Program to document client vehicles received for student-supervised instructional service. The form captures vehicle details, requested maintenance or repair items via a multi-point inspection checklist, walk-around comments, and a critical liability waiver section that educates clients that work is performed by students under instructor supervision, disclaims any warranty, and releases the district from liability for damage, faulty workmanship, or lost property. It also specifies that clients must provide their own parts and that unclaimed vehicles may be towed after 7 calendar days.

- **entities:** `Dallas Independent School District`, `Greater Dallas Workforce Development Area 6`, `TEAL`, `CTER Perkins Summary`, `CTER Supplemental Report`, `Career Institute North`, `Career Institute South`, `Career Institute East`, `Skyline High School`, `Townview Education Center`, `Carter High School`, `Lincoln High School`
- **artifacts:** `clna-draft`, `needs-assessment`, `performance-tracker`, `data-document`, `program-evaluation`, `labor-market-alignment`
- **summary:** This is the Dallas ISD draft Comprehensive Local Needs Assessment (CLNA) for the 2026-2027 Perkins V grant cycle, prepared in May 2026. It structures the district's CTE needs across five performance lines—student outcomes, LEA baseline review, special population equity, gender/race/ethnicity gaps, and labor market alignment—with detailed submission text and supporting analysis for each. The document introduces Local Levels of Performance (LLPs), tracks Career Institute expansion to close Health Science and Architecture/Construction enrollment gaps, and identifies specific student groups (EB/EL, economically disadvantaged, African American females, and males) requiring targeted interventions. It connects Perkins allocation data, STAAR/CCMR outcomes, and regional LMI projections to inform budget and program decisions.

- **summary:** This document presents Dallas ISD's Comprehensive Work-Based Learning Continuum, a K-12 framework bridging classroom instruction and industry experience. It outlines grade-specific WBL activities from middle school career awareness through 12th-grade practicums and internships, organized across Dallas ISD's 14 CTE career clusters. The plan emphasizes virtual engagement, industry partnerships, and postsecondary transition skills, serving as a guide for teachers and administrators to structure student career development from exploration to job readiness.

### `Dallas ISD — NAF Membership Agreement 2026-27[57].pdf`

- **tags:** `naf-membership-agreement`, `naf-membership-2026-27`, `dallas-isd-cte`, `academy-membership`, `core-membership-fee`, `year-of-planning`, `fast-track`, `knope`, `naf-track-certification`, `advisory-board`, `work-based-learning`, `curriculum-alignment`, `emerging-academies`, `existing-academies`, `2026-27-school-year`, `naf-design-standards`
- **entities:** `Dallas Independent School District`, `Bryan Adams Leadership Academy`, `Emmet J. Conrad High School`, `Hillcrest High School`, `Justin F. Kimball High School`, `L.G. Pinkston High School`, `North Dallas High School`, `Skyline High School`, `Sunset High School`, `Thomas Jefferson High School`, `W.H. Adamson High School`, `W.T. White High School`
- **artifacts:** `membership-agreement`, `service-contract`, `policy-document`, `academy-authorization`, `budget-planning`, `program-of-study`
- **summary:** This nine-page NAF Membership Agreement formalizes the partnership between Dallas ISD and the National Academy Foundation for the 2026-27 school year, governing 20 existing and 3 emerging CTE academies across the district. The agreement outlines core membership fees of $1,750 per academy, optional Year of Planning ($12,000) and Fast Track ($4,000) processes for new academies, and details academy responsibilities including curriculum implementation, advisory board establishment, work-based learning sequences, and data reporting through the NAF Quality Center. It also covers optional extended services, NAFTrack Certification, and pilot programs like KnoPro.

### `DallasISDTravel - ACTE Best Practices - JS.pdf`

- **tags:** `travel-form`, `travel-request`, `expense-report`, `arta-travel`, `conference-travel`, `employee-travel`, `dallas-isd-travel`, `vendor-quote`, `trip-planning`, `hotel-booking`, `car-rental`, `flight-booking`

- **summary:** This document is the official Dallas ISD travel request form used by employees and staff to plan and request approved travel, including conferences and professional development events. It captures personal information, trip details (dates, destinations, transportation preferences), and hotel/airline/car rental preferences. The form directs users to submit requests via email to ARTA Travel for booking and quotes, after which they must reconcile expenses through Oracle iExpense. It serves as the primary gateway for authorized district travel and ensures compliance with district vendor policies.

- **tags:** `perkins-v-clna`, `dallas-isd-cte`, `2026-2027-comprehensive-local-needs-assessment`, `clna-draft-coordinator-review`, `part-1-application-designation`, `part-2-student-performance`, `part-3-labor-market-alignment`, `llp-local-levels-of-performance`, `wda-6-greater-dallas`, `career-institutes-cte`, `reading-ela-proficiency-gap`, `math-proficiency-gap`, `science-proficiency-gap`, `post-program-placement-gap`, `non-traditional-concentration`, `postsecondary-credential-gap`, `special-population-performance`, `eb-ell-population-shift`, `swd-underperformance`, `gender-race-ethnicity-performance`
- **entities:** `Dallas Independent School District`, `WDA 6 Greater Dallas`, `TEAL CTER Perkins`, `Class of 2025`, `Class of 2026`, `Career Institute North`, `Career Institute South`, `Career Institute East`, `Townview Ed`, `Townview Health`, `Carter`, `Lincoln`
- **artifacts:** `comprehensive-local-needs-assessment-draft`, `clna-workbook`, `perkins-application-support`, `performance-data-tracker`, `labor-market-alignment-analysis`, `student-performance-submission-form`, `special-population-comparison`, `gender-race-ethnicity-analysis`
- **summary:** This is the 2026-2027 Dallas ISD Comprehensive Local Needs Assessment (CLNA) draft prepared for Perkins V coordinator review. It organizes CLNA work across five parts—application designation, student performance, labor market alignment, special population performance, and gender/race/ethnicity analysis—with submission text and supporting analysis for each line. The draft flags multiple performance gaps (Reading/ELA, Math, Science, post-program placement, postsecondary credential, non-traditional concentration) and demographic shifts (notably EB/EL rising from 40.6% to 58.3%). It introduces Local Levels of Performance (LLPs) as a new framework, tracks CTE vs. non-CTE comparisons, and identifies alignment gaps between top LMI clusters (Health Science, Business/Finance, IT, Architecture/Construction) and current enrollment, with Career Institutes positioned as the primary strategy for closing top gaps. The document requires TEAL/CTER data insertion, coordinator confirmation, and verification of public data before final submission.

- **summary:** This September 11, 2024 Federal Register notice from the U.S. Department of Education’s Office of Career, Technical, and Adult Education proposes revisions to the Perkins V State Plan Guide and Consolidated Annual Report (CAR) information collections. The changes aim to align CTE performance measurement with statutory requirements by establishing clearer data specifications for numerators and denominators, addressing discrepancies between CTE concentrator identification and ESEA assessment timing, and shifting from active-cohort to exit-cohort reporting for academic proficiency indicators. The notice solicits public comment on these proposed changes to improve data consistency and accuracy across states.

The guide serves as a compliance and planning reference for CTE administrators at state and local levels.

### `Documents/Conference Travel/CTE CTSO Sponsorship Guidelines 25-26.docx.pdf`

- **tags:** `ctso-sponsorship-guidelines-25-26`, `career-technical-student-organizations`, `ctso-governance-and-compliance`, `ctso-stipend-structure-2025-26`, `ctso-officer-and-membership-tracking`, `ctso-fundraising-and-community-service`, `ctso-travel-and-conference-requirements`, `dallas-isd-cte-policy-manual`, `ctso-chapter-requirements-2025-26`, `ctso-value-added-payments`
- **entities:** `Business Professionals of America`, `DECA`, `Future Business Leaders of America`, `Family Career and Community Leaders of America`, `FFA`, `HOSA`, `SkillsUSA`, `Texas Association of Future Educators`, `Technology Student Association`, `Eduthings`, `Cornerstone`
- **artifacts:** `policy-guideline-document`, `stipend-and-compliance-manual`, `sponsor-agreement-template`, `tracking-checklist`
- **summary:** This document is the official Dallas ISD CTE CTSO Sponsorship Guidelines for the 2025-26 school year, governing how Career and Technical Student Organizations are sponsored, funded, and tracked across the district. It outlines sponsor responsibilities, eligibility requirements for base and value-added stipends, mandatory documentation deadlines, and chapter compliance standards (bylaws, meetings, leadership, community service). The guidelines are endorsed by USDOE, TEA, and Dallas ISD, and apply to all nine state-affiliated CTSOs. Sponsors must submit documentation via Eduthings to receive payments tied to student membership, competitions, and program activities.

### `Documents/Conference Travel/CTE CTSO Sponsorship Guidelines 25-26.pdf`

- **tags:** `ctso-sponsorship-guidelines-25-26`, `ctso-base-stipend`, `ctso-value-added-payments`, `ctso-chapter-requirements`, `ctso-sponsor-responsibilities`, `ctso-documentation-deadlines`, `ctso-2025-26`, `ctso-stipend-structure`, `ctso-officer-list`, `ctso-meetings-minutes`, `ctso-community-service`, `ctso-fundraising`, `ctso-travel-paperwork`, `ctso-membership-registration`, `ctso-bylaws`, `ctso-program-of-work`, `ctso-competition-qualifiers`, `ctso-region-state-district`, `ctso-sponsor-agreement`, `ctso-eduthings-submission`
- **entities:** `Business Professionals of America (BPA)`, `DECA`, `Future Business Leaders of America (FBLA)`, `Family Career and Community Leaders of America (FCCLA)`, `Future Farmers of America (FFA)`, `Health Occupations Students of America (HOSA)`, `SkillsUSA`, `Texas Association of Future Educators (TAFE)`, `Technology Student Association (TSA)`, `Texas Education Agency (TEA)`, `United States Department of Education (USDOE)`, `Dallas ISD`

- **summary:** This document provides the official Dallas ISD guidelines for Career and Technical Student Organization (CTSO) sponsorship during the 2025-26 school year. It outlines sponsor responsibilities, eligibility requirements for base and value-added stipends, mandatory documentation deadlines, and chapter standards (bylaws, meetings, community service, fundraising, and competition participation). The guidelines are endorsed by USDOE, TEA, and Dallas ISD, and list the nine recognized CTSOs. Sponsors must submit documentation via Eduthings to qualify for $500 base stipends (tiered by membership and sponsor count) and $250 value-added payments for state/national competition attendance and supervision.

- **summary:** This document is a Section 504 Student Review Committee Report and Placement Notice for Mackenna Spence, an 11th grade student at Martin High School in Arlington ISD. The initial 504 plan was established on 04/27/2026 based on evaluations diagnosing her with ADHD and Anxiety, which substantially limit concentrating and thinking. The committee recommended placement in regular education with accommodations including breaking down large assignments, frequent breaks, and sitting near instruction. Input was gathered from teachers, parents, the student, and a counselor. The document also includes required Texas Driving with Disability Program information for students age 16 and older.

### `NICHQ Teacher.pdf`

- **tags:** `vanderbilt-assessment`, `teacher-informant`, `nichq-form`, `adhd-screening`, `pediatric-behavioral-health`, `classroom-performance-rating`, `symptom-rating-scale`, `2002-aap-form`, `assessment-continuation`, `teacher-evaluation-form`
- **entities:** `NICHQ Vanderbilt Assessment Scale`, `American Academy of Pediatrics`, `National Initiative for Children's Healthcare Quality`
- **artifacts:** `assessment-form`, `rating-scale`, `teacher-worksheet`, `medical-screening-form`
- **summary:** This is the NICHQ Vanderbilt Assessment Scale—Teacher Informant, a standardized pediatric behavioral health screening tool used by teachers to rate student symptoms of attention deficit and hyperactivity, as well as academic and classroom performance. Published by the American Academy of Pediatrics in 2002 and adapted from Dr. Mark L. Wolraich's Vanderbilt Rating Scales, the form collects symptom ratings across 43 items, performance scores, and optional comments. It is designed for classroom use and is typically returned to a mailing address or fax number for clinical or administrative follow-up.

- **summary:** This PDF is the official 2026-2027 Texas Perkins V Comprehensive Local Needs Assessment (CLNA) Guidebook, published January 31, 2026. It provides structured guidance and a step-by-step process for local education agencies to evaluate their CTE systems, covering student performance, labor market alignment, program quality, equity of access, educator recruitment, and alignment with the local Perkins application. The guidebook is designed to be used on a two-year cycle and requires stakeholder advisory committee input to complete each section and set priorities for future Perkins spending.

- **summary:** This document defines the Texas CTE indicator coding framework used to classify secondary students into categories such as Not CTE, Participants, Explorers, Concentrators, and Completers based on course enrollment, credit completion, and program of study requirements under 19 TAC Chapters 126, 127, and 130. It includes special rules for regional programs of study outside approved ESC geographic regions, where students are recoded as Explorers. The framework is used for state accountability, reporting, and district-level CTE compliance and funding determinations.
